Structural Blindness: Primac<sub>y</sub> and Recenc<sub>y</sub> Efects. Be<sub>y</sub>ond a<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>ate metrics<sub>,</sub> our dia<sub>g</sub>nostic <sub>p</sub>robes reveal that model failures are rooted in <sub>p</sub>ositional as<sub>y</sub>mmetries inherent to transformer architectures [18, 36, 71]. Man<sub>y</sub> models show dis<sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>ortionate reliance <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t f<sub>rame</sub> t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>ne overa</sub>ll <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>y,</sub> i<sub>gnor</sub>i<sub>ng su</sub>b<sub>sequen</sub>t lo<sub>g</sub>ical contradictions (<sub>p</sub>rimac<sub>y</sub> bias [62]); conversel<sub>y</sub>, middlesequence information is often invisible to the judge, (recency bias and lost-in-the-middle efect [36]), artifacts of causal maskin<sub>g</sub> and RoPE [55] not o<sub>p</sub>timized for the bidirectional lo<sub>g</sub>ic re<sub>q</sub>uired b<sub>y</sub> interleaved stor<sub>y</sub>tellin<sub>g</sub> [24, 53]. Criticall<sub>y</sub>, these as<sub>y</sub>mmetries <sub>p</sub>ersist <sub>even a</sub>ft<sub>er</sub> fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>un</sub>i<sub>ng, con</sub>fi<sub>rm</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t SFT <sub>conso</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> miti<sub>g</sub>ates structural bias [59].

While ada<sub>p</sub>tation throu<sub>g</sub>h multi-<sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t tr<sub>a</sub>inin<sub>g</sub> $\scriptstyle ( \mathrm { O n e V i s i o n ^ { \frac { 1 } { \cdot } } } )$ <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gn</sub>ifi<sub>can</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> b<sub>o</sub>l<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ers seman</sub>ti<sub>c ou</sub>tli<sub>er</sub> d<sub>e-</sub> tection $( F _ { 1 } { = } 0 . 5 5 , \mathrm { A c c = } 0 . 8 2 )$ <sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> PRISM-T<sub>e</sub>m<sub>po</sub>r<sub>a</sub>l <sub>su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t r<sub>e</sub>m<sub>a</sub>in<sub>s</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>eng</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub> All <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>s</sub> f<sub>a</sub>ll b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow</sub> th<sub>e</sub> $F _ { 1 } { = } 0 . 4 0$ threshold<sub>,</sub> with SFT <sub>p</sub>rovidin<sub>g</sub> onl<sub>y</sub> mar<sub>g</sub>inal <sub>g</sub>ains over zero-shot baselines. The failure of the Analyze-then-Judge paradigm to shift results on this subset confirms that tem<sub>p</sub>oral orderin<sub>g</sub> sensitivit<sub>y</sub> is <sub>a</sub> <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>capa</sub>bilit<sub>y,</sub> l<sub>arge</sub>l<sub>y</sub> d<sub>ecoup</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>d reasonin<sub>g</sub> su<sub>p</sub>ervision [23].

SFT-Ind<sub>uce</sub>d Shift<sub>s</sub> in D<sub>o</sub>m<sub>a</sub>in Tr<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y.</sub> Fin<sub>e</sub>-t<sub>u</sub>nin<sub>g o</sub>n MI-RAGE efecti<sub>v</sub>el<sub>y</sub> in<sub>v</sub>erts the zero-shot tractabilit<sub>y</sub> rankin<sub>g</sub> of s<sub>u</sub>bdomains (Table S3). Without ada<sub>p</sub>tation, MIRAGE-Recipes a<sub>pp</sub>ears m<sub>o</sub>r<sub>e</sub> tr<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e;</sub> h<sub>oweve</sub>r<sub>,</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ow</sub>in<sub>g</sub> SFT<sub>,</sub> MIRAGE-Vist <sub>e</sub>m<sub>e</sub>r<sub>ges</sub> <sub>as</sub> the su<sub>p</sub>erior subset (Acc 0.92 vs. 0.87). This su<sub>gg</sub>ests that ada<sub>p</sub>tati<sub>o</sub>n i<sub>s</sub> m<sub>o</sub>r<sub>e e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ve a</sub>t r<sub>eso</sub>l<sub>v</sub>in<sub>g</sub> n<sub>a</sub>rr<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve a</sub>mbi<sub>gu</sub>it<sub>y</sub> in <sub>v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l stor<sub>y</sub>tellin<sub>g</sub> than at overcomin<sub>g</sub> the ri<sub>g</sub>id <sub>p</sub>rocedural constraints of reci<sub>p</sub>e-based hallucinations [44].

Converse<sup>l</sup>y, PRISM Tem<sub>p</sub>oral exhibits a recenc<sub>y</sub> efect<sub>,</sub> where swa<sub>p</sub>s at later <sub>p</sub>ositions <sub>are marg</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ll<sub>y eas</sub>i<sub>er</sub> t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>, errors</sub> b<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e m</sub>iddl<sub>e o</sub>f <sub>a</sub> 5<sub>–</sub>6 ima<sub>g</sub>e se<sub>q</sub>uence are fre<sub>q</sub>uentl<sub>y</sub> invisible to the jud<sub>g</sub>e [36].
